- The five [engineering capacities](concepts.md#c12) as descriptive tools (3.2).
- Descriptive-layer [compression](concepts.md#c31) when the carrier is finite (3.4).
- Reading one [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18) as: there are [arrivals](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10) on both sides of the boundary, and at least one side regards more than one [arrival](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10) as one (5.1).
- "All the history that arrives at it participates in weight" in the definition of [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) is read as holding at every [moment](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c09) (9.3).
- The [cut](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c23) on which the judgment about [current artificial intelligence](concepts.md#c11) rests, and that it can perform the acts and retention measured by the five [engineering capacities](concepts.md#c12) (9.2).
- The answers to the four questions state only four kinds of thing, pathways, necessary conditions, no guarantee and unconfirmability, and no sufficient conditions (1.2).
- The persons this paper discusses are recorded as [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16): by the [Application Assumption](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c50) they are assumed not to be [locked in](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c12), and all the [history](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c13) that [arrives](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10) at them is assumed to participate in their [weight](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c06) (1.1, 9.1).
- Between machines of the same [structure](concepts.md#c07) whose constraints are determined only by [structure](concepts.md#c07) and state, the state can be fully read out and copied (9.6).
- The constraints of different ranges are not assumed to be the same: that two [cases](concepts.md#c24) use the same set of constraints means that the same input has the same [weight](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c06) distribution in the two [cases](concepts.md#c24), and is a condition to be stated separately (1.1, 5.5).
- How the complexity a network can sustain changes is not derived from the capacities of [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) alone: population, division of labor, resources, the maintenance of [signal containers](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c26) and [templates](../paper_2ccbf179_58cf_4851_bd72_5fa886e4b2c3/concepts.md#c14), and the density of [arrivals](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10) all take part; this paper does not deal with the network layer (8.6).

To negate them means to switch to another set of settings.

<note>

*These are this paper's settings, not claims meant to be overturned. Not accepting them means writing with another set of settings.*

</note>

### 10.2 Empirical Premises

[Empirical Premises](concepts.md#c01) One to Three are written in the form of [empirical prohibition](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c51): they state only that without these it [never occurs](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c01), not that with them it occurs; they are calibrated from cases and can be revised by new cases. For [Empirical Premises](concepts.md#c01) Four and Five, see their notes.

1. **[Empirical Premise](concepts.md#c01) One**: without retaining some copy of its own [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) (belonging to [memory capacity](concepts.md#c15)), or without checking the copy against subsequent [arrivals](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10) (belonging to [operation capacity](concepts.md#c14)), there is no [self-other discrimination](concepts.md#c48) (6.3). Calibration: the reafference principle, crickets, tickling.
2. **[Empirical Premise](concepts.md#c01) Two**: without retention across different [moments](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c09) (belonging to [memory capacity](concepts.md#c15)), or without [compressing](concepts.md#c31) the [arrivals](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10) caused by oneself at different [moments](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c09) into one class (belonging to [compression capacity](concepts.md#c16)), there is no [cross-moment lumping](concepts.md#c49) (6.3). Calibration: mirror self-recognition.
3. **[Empirical Premise](concepts.md#c01) Three**: without retention across [moments](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c09) (belonging to [memory capacity](concepts.md#c15)), the content of a [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) cannot take part in organizing [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) when the [stimulus](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c30) is not present (7.2).
4. **[Empirical Premise](concepts.md#c01) Four**: on certain dimensions stated by the researcher, the [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) of [current artificial intelligence](concepts.md#c11) cannot be distinguished from human [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) (9.1). This one differs from the three above: it is not a necessary condition but a registration of existing test results; by Proposition Three, it reaches only the registered tests and can be overturned by new tests made on one of these dimensions under the same criteria, and if overturned, the two points that on that dimension it is a [simulation](concepts.md#c51) and is [functionally equivalent](concepts.md#c52) (9.1), and the parts of 9.5, 9.8, 11.4 and Section 13 that rely on it, lose their source; the judgment that it is not a [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) is not affected.
5. **[Empirical Premise](concepts.md#c01) Five**: the [outputs](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) of a [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) that has a [self-maintained filtering boundary](concepts.md#c47) lean toward maintaining the inside of the boundary (1.5). Like [Empirical Premise](concepts.md#c01) Four, this one is not a necessary condition; it is induced from the cases observed so far, and if among new cases there is such a [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) whose [outputs](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) do not lean toward maintaining the inside of the boundary, that overturns it; if overturned it affects only the researcher's summary "it is maintaining itself". Calibration: cells repairing their own cell membranes.

<note>

*Empirical Premises One to Three are necessary conditions generalized from cases: without a copy to compare, what one did oneself cannot be told apart; without remembering oneself at different times, there is no continuing self; without remembering, a construct cannot be used when the thing is not in front of one. The fourth is different: it only records current test results, that in some respects the output of artificial intelligence cannot be told apart from a person's. New tests may overturn it; if overturned, it affects only the two points "it counts as a simulation in that respect, and the effect is the same", and the later parts that rely on them, not "it is not a Wo". The fifth is generalized from observations: what things with their own boundaries, such as cells, do mostly leans toward maintaining the inside of the boundary; new observations may overturn it, and if overturned it affects only the summary "it is maintaining itself".*

</note>

### 10.3 Failure Conditions

- When some [empirical premise](concepts.md#c01) is overturned by new cases, the conclusions whose source chain contains that premise lose their source, and what remains is only the cases already observed and the tests already registered; losing the source only means the chain of derivation is broken, not that the conclusion is false.
- This paper's derivations rely on the upstream [Law of Flux](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c24), [Law of the Black Box](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c25), [Law of Attrition](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c26) and [Law of Non-Restoration](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c28), and on the definitions of [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16), [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18), [arrival](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10), [history](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c13), [lock-in](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c12), [computation](../paper_fb0addca_42b6_4884_aa18_438c695747ab/concepts.md#c05), [decision](../paper_fb0addca_42b6_4884_aa18_438c695747ab/concepts.md#c06), [template](../paper_2ccbf179_58cf_4851_bd72_5fa886e4b2c3/concepts.md#c14), [signal container](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c26), [resonance](../paper_2ccbf179_58cf_4851_bd72_5fa886e4b2c3/concepts.md#c09) and others; whether they hold is handled upstream.
- The conclusions that rely on the premise of 2.3 (2.1, 4.3, 6.2, 6.3, 7.1, 7.7, 8.4, 11.2, 11.4, and Section 13, which summarizes them) lose their source when that premise is negated; the negation is: some [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18) or some [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) of a [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) does not [arrive](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10) at that [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) itself before the next [moment](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c09). After losing the source, these conclusions no longer have a source for [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) in general; what remains are only the situations in which the [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18) and [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) do come back to oneself, which can be judged separately; losing the source only means the chain of derivation is broken, not that the conclusion is false; conclusions that do not rely on that premise are not affected.

<note>

*When an empirical premise is overturned, the conclusions derived from it lose their grounds, and only the cases already seen and the tests already done remain, but that does not mean the conclusions are wrong. Whether the upstream laws and definitions this paper uses hold is the upstream's responsibility. The few conclusions that rely on the premise "distinction comes back to oneself" also lose their grounds once that premise is negated (some distinction or output does not come back to oneself), and only the situations where distinction and output do come back to oneself remain to be judged separately; conclusions that do not use this premise are not affected.*

</note>

---

## 11. What Cannot Be Derived

For rows whose right column says "necessarily", the reason is the definition of [not guaranteed](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c02): the framework does not assert that it necessarily happens.

<note>

*For the rows in the tables that say "necessarily", the reason is always the same: this framework never asserts that anything will certainly happen. For some other rows, the reason is written in parentheses in the right column.*

</note>


### 11.1 Overall

| Known | Cannot be derived |
|---|---|
| The researcher describes [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) with [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) C | The studied [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) has C inside |
| Party A says [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) C, and it [arrives](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10) at party B | Party B understands, believes or adopts C |
| Party B later changes its [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) | C is the only cause (the whole [history](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c13) that [arrives](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10) at party B participates in its [weight](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c06), the definition of [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16)) |
| The same word | The same inner [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18) or the same [output tendency](../paper_81952268_47b1_4d8c_9605_270c42646693/concepts.md#c02) |
| The researcher renames [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) C as D | The physical [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) of the [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) concerned changes accordingly |
| New behavior or invention appears | There exists an additional "inventive generative force" |
| Recursion has no external starting point | Each result is already determined, or [history](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c13) can be rerun ([Law of the Black Box](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c25), [Law of Non-Restoration](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c28)) |
| The object of an [illusory construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) is not given by the physical world | The signal carrying it is not a physical event |
| All necessary conditions are present | The result necessarily occurs |

<note>

*Overall table: a researcher describing with a word does not mean the other has it in mind; saying a word that reaches others does not mean they accept it; others changing afterwards does not mean it was only because of that sentence; the same word does not mean the same meaning; a researcher renaming does not change what the other did; something new appearing does not mean an extra creative power; having no external starting point does not mean the result was settled long ago; what a construct points to is not given by physics, but the sounds and writing carrying it are physical; all conditions being present does not mean it will certainly happen.*

</note>

### 11.2 Question One: Function

| Known | Cannot be derived |
|---|---|
| A [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) has some [function](concepts.md#c18) | The [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) appeared because of it, or has a purpose |
| There is no [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) | The content of [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18) does not participate in [weight](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c06) |
| There is a [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) and [inference](concepts.md#c36) is possible | The [inference](concepts.md#c36) agrees with later [arrivals](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10) |
| In the examples, [constructs](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) appear in [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) only when [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) is to [arrive](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10) at others | When nothing is said, this [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) has no such [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) inside |

<note>

*A construct being useful does not mean it appeared for the sake of that use. Without constructs, experience still works. Being able to infer does not mean inferring accurately. In the examples, constructs are spoken only when there is someone to talk to, which does not mean the person lacks them inside when nothing is said.*

</note>

### 11.3 Engineering Capacities and Commutativity

| Known | Cannot be derived |
|---|---|
| This paper selects five [engineering capacities](concepts.md#c12) | Something is a [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) only if it has all five, or there are only these five |
| The definition of [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) | There is a findable [representation](concepts.md#c34) on the carrier |
| Quantitative indicators chosen by the researcher | The dimensions of the capacity itself |
| Describing reality in terms of dimensions or data rates | The dimensions and bit rate of reality are already quantifiable |
| Persisting | The stronger the capacity, the better the persistence |
| Some capacity below some degree | Cannot persist within a stated time window |
| [Compression](concepts.md#c31) groups different information into fewer [representations](concepts.md#c34) | [Compression](concepts.md#c31) brings its own material, or every [compression](concepts.md#c31) forms a [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) |
| Higher [operation capacity](concepts.md#c14) | Making up differences lost in containment |
| A set of [inference rules](concepts.md#c44) is [self-consistent](concepts.md#c45) | It is [commutative within a range](concepts.md#c42) |
| A [compression](concepts.md#c31) is [commutative](concepts.md#c55) | Its [inference rules](concepts.md#c44) are [self-consistent](concepts.md#c45) |
| There are exact [inference rules](concepts.md#c44) for a [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) not [locked in](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c12), and the results it may produce differ by more than twice the [allowed error](concepts.md#c56) in the [later representation](concepts.md#c33) | Single results are [commutative](concepts.md#c55) |
| A [compression](concepts.md#c31) is [commutative](concepts.md#c55) within some range | It is [commutative](concepts.md#c55) within all ranges |
| A [compression](concepts.md#c31) has so far passed [outcome feedback](../paper_2ccbf179_58cf_4851_bd72_5fa886e4b2c3/concepts.md#c05) | It has been proved to have chosen the right dimensions |
| The [representations](concepts.md#c34) of two [cases](concepts.md#c24) are the same | They are the same [target](concepts.md#c25) |
| A wrong [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) is observed | It is necessarily a lack of [structure](concepts.md#c07) |
| An engineering model uses the word "weight" | Upstream [branch](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c05) [weight](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c06) has been measured |

<note>

*What cannot be derived in this part on engineering capacities and commutativity: the five capacities are not the conditions of being a Wo, nor all of its capacities; there being a Wo does not mean traces can be found; scores are not the capacity itself; surviving does not mean strong capacity, and weak capacity does not mean failing to survive; compression does not conjure up material, nor does it produce a construct every time; calculating fast cannot make up differences that could not be told apart; being free of internal contradiction does not mean fitting what arrives later, and vice versa; for something not locked in, however exact the rules, a single result is not guaranteed accurate; fitting within one range does not mean fitting everywhere; fitting up to now does not mean it has been proved right; two cases having the same representation does not mean they are the same thing; a wrong answer is not necessarily a problem of structure; what engineering calls "weight" is not the upstream weight.*

</note>

### 11.4 Question Two: Generation

| Known | Cannot be derived |
|---|---|
| The researcher [compresses](concepts.md#c31) a [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16)'s [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) with "self" | That [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) defines itself by [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18) |
| There is [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18) | There is a [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) |
| There is no name | There is no [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) |
| A [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18) uses no [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) at the moment it is made | Its content will not later become a [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) |
| All the conditions on the rungs of the ladder are present | It is a [self](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c19) |
| There is a [self-maintained filtering boundary](concepts.md#c47) | It has made a self-definition |
| The researcher assumes [self](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c19) at the human scale | The human is the "true self" |
| [Representations](concepts.md#c34) can be processed again | [Representations](concepts.md#c34) become ever more numerous and ever more correct, or necessarily lead to [second-order distinction](../paper_fb0addca_42b6_4884_aa18_438c695747ab/concepts.md#c01) |
| Having the five [engineering capacities](concepts.md#c12) | There is [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18) |
| A [node](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c25)'s [computation](../paper_fb0addca_42b6_4884_aa18_438c695747ab/concepts.md#c05) is read by the researcher as a [commutative](concepts.md#c55) [compression](concepts.md#c31) | This [node](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c25) has [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18) |
| [Output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) is similar to that of a [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) | It is a [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) |
| [Functionally equivalent](concepts.md#c52) to a [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) on some dimensions | It is a [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16), or is [functionally equivalent](concepts.md#c52) on all dimensions |
| [Output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) is read as carrying content, for example a name | The [node](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c25) producing it has a [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) that is the content of [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18) |
| The content of [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18) participates in later [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) | There is a [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) (participation itself has no object) |
| A content has been said only once | It is not a [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) |
| Grouping different things into one class | There is a [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) (this is [lumping](concepts.md#c23) within [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18)) |
| The [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) of [current artificial intelligence](concepts.md#c11) describes a [cut](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c23) | [Current artificial intelligence](concepts.md#c11) has made the [cut](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c23) |
| [Current artificial intelligence](concepts.md#c11) is sometimes [locked in](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c12) | It is therefore not a [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) (humans are also [locked in](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c12) at times) |
| The [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) of [current artificial intelligence](concepts.md#c11) changes when it is run | Parameters are updated after each input (with random [rounds](../paper_fb0addca_42b6_4884_aa18_438c695747ab/concepts.md#c04), single results can differ, 9.6) |
| [Current artificial intelligence](concepts.md#c11) grows in scale | Intelligence necessarily increases monotonically, or [current artificial intelligence](concepts.md#c11) becomes a [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) (whether it is a [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) is judged by design and [cut](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c23), 9.2, 9.3; having [engineering capacities](concepts.md#c12) does not imply [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18), 9.7) |
| Two machines of the same [structure](concepts.md#c07) can copy their states | Two [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) can become copies of each other through [arrival](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10) |

<note>

*What cannot be derived in this part on generation: a researcher saying it is "maintaining itself" does not mean it has itself settled on a "self"; having distinction does not mean having constructs, and having no name does not mean having no constructs; not using a construct at the moment does not mean it cannot become one later; having all the conditions on the ladder does not mean being a "self"; having a cell membrane does not mean self-definition; putting "self" on the human does not mean the human is the true self; representations being reprocessable does not mean they will become ever more numerous and ever more correct; however strong the engineering capacities and however accurate the calculation, none of this means there is distinction; being much like a person, or having the same effect in some respects, does not mean being a Wo; saying a name does not mean having a construct inside; experience at work, something said only once, grouping things into a class: none of these can be used to judge whether there is a construct; artificial intelligence writing out a cut does not mean it made the cut; sometimes being locked in is not the reason it is not a Wo; output changing when it is run does not mean parameters are being updated; growing in scale does not mean becoming a Wo; machines being able to copy their states does not mean people can.*

</note>

### 11.5 Question Three: Use

| Known | Cannot be derived |
|---|---|
| Obtaining a [template](../paper_2ccbf179_58cf_4851_bd72_5fa886e4b2c3/concepts.md#c14) | Obtaining its scope of application, or the obtainer's execution being the same as others' |
| The parties' [outputs](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) applying a [template](../paper_2ccbf179_58cf_4851_bd72_5fa886e4b2c3/concepts.md#c14) are in [resonance](../paper_2ccbf179_58cf_4851_bd72_5fa886e4b2c3/concepts.md#c09) | The parties' [compressions](concepts.md#c31) are the same |
| Some content is a [template](../paper_2ccbf179_58cf_4851_bd72_5fa886e4b2c3/concepts.md#c14) | It passed through some [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16)'s [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) |
| There is a [template](../paper_2ccbf179_58cf_4851_bd72_5fa886e4b2c3/concepts.md#c14) in the [record](../paper_81952268_47b1_4d8c_9605_270c42646693/concepts.md#c06) | The [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) that [explicitly listed](concepts.md#c09) it has a [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) inside |
| A network has [culture](../paper_2ccbf179_58cf_4851_bd72_5fa886e4b2c3/concepts.md#c13) | The participants have [constructs](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01), [templates](../paper_2ccbf179_58cf_4851_bd72_5fa886e4b2c3/concepts.md#c14) or [second-order distinction](../paper_fb0addca_42b6_4884_aa18_438c695747ab/concepts.md#c01) |
| [Templates](../paper_2ccbf179_58cf_4851_bd72_5fa886e4b2c3/concepts.md#c14) can be used repeatedly within [culture](../paper_2ccbf179_58cf_4851_bd72_5fa886e4b2c3/concepts.md#c13) | [Culture](../paper_2ccbf179_58cf_4851_bd72_5fa886e4b2c3/concepts.md#c13) must pass through a [template](../paper_2ccbf179_58cf_4851_bd72_5fa886e4b2c3/concepts.md#c14) stage, or [templates](../paper_2ccbf179_58cf_4851_bd72_5fa886e4b2c3/concepts.md#c14) are a layer of [culture](../paper_2ccbf179_58cf_4851_bd72_5fa886e4b2c3/concepts.md#c13) |
| Two [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) repeatedly [arrive](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10) at each other | [Culture](../paper_2ccbf179_58cf_4851_bd72_5fa886e4b2c3/concepts.md#c13) necessarily forms, or [weight](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c06) necessarily converges |
| Certain compatible [outputs](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) recur | The inner [weight](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c06) of a [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) has been measured, or a compression rate has been proved ([comparison](concepts.md#c38) reaches only [cases](concepts.md#c24) that have [arrived](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10), Proposition Three) |
| Not remembering the content of a [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18) | Being unable to make a [second-order distinction](../paper_fb0addca_42b6_4884_aa18_438c695747ab/concepts.md#c01) about it |
| Ordinary adults use the relevant [constructs](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) | Their second-order inner [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18) has been verified |

<note>

*What cannot be derived in this part on use: getting the rules does not mean getting their scope, nor that everyone follows them in the same way; what people do fitting together does not mean their compressions are the same; there being a set of rules does not mean the writer has a construct inside; there being culture does not mean people have constructs or rules; rules can be used again and again within culture, but culture need not pass through rules; frequent interaction does not mean culture necessarily forms; repeatedly fitting together does not mean weight has been measured; from having forgotten a certain thought, it cannot be derived that one cannot look back on it; adults often saying "we" does not prove they have second-order distinction.*

</note>

### 11.6 Question Four: After Use

| Known | Cannot be derived |
|---|---|
| A [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) is not [commutative](concepts.md#c55) | It ceases to be used |
| Disagreeing material [arrives](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10) | The [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) is therefore abandoned |
| A configuration with [second-order capacity](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c02) | Necessarily higher social complexity, necessarily invention or understanding |
| The five capacities are strong enough | Necessarily forming [second-order distinction](../paper_fb0addca_42b6_4884_aa18_438c695747ab/concepts.md#c01), understanding, or highly complex networks |
| A larger population | Necessarily higher complexity of civilization |
| Systematic [lumping](concepts.md#c23) appears | The boundary is drawn wrongly (all [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18) contains [lumping](concepts.md#c23), 5.1; the same stretch of the [universe](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c02) may be drawn as one or as many, and either holds, 2.1) |

<note>

*What cannot be derived after use: a construct that does not fit is not necessarily dropped; having second-order capacity or very strong capacities does not mean society will certainly be more complex or that there will certainly be inventions; more people does not mean civilization will certainly be more complex; everyone grouping things this way does not mean the line is drawn wrongly.*

</note>

## 12. Cases

This whole section serves only as calibration, and is not a source for any derivation. The data are taken from public web pages, not original documents; where sources attribute causes to the same event, these are marked as the sources' attributions.

<note>

*The two cases in this section are only calibration, not evidence. The data are all extracted from web pages, not original documents.*

</note>

### 12.1 Children Learning "You" and "I"

**What happened.**

English-speaking children fully master personal pronouns only at about three years old. In the course of learning, some children reverse "you" and "I". This is not common: most children do not reverse them, and even those who err are rarely consistent; in one sample, of 30 early-talking children around one year and eight months old, 17 reversed them at least once during two hours of observation.

The [Providence Corpus](concepts.md#c57) is a long-term recording of six English-speaking children's conversations with their parents at home; two of the children in it reversed them for several months in a row. The girl Naima, from about one year seven months to two years four months, would say "you have blueberries on your face", meaning on her own face. Overall she reversed 79% of second-person pronouns (243 out of 306), and only 3% of first-person ones (13 out of 477). At two years five months the reversals stopped abruptly, with no transition period; the authors speculate that this may be related to a family trip, or may be a developmental change around two years four months, and say the two explanations are not mutually exclusive. The other child, the boy Ethan, reversed especially often when imitating his mother's speech; when the data end, he was still reversing.

Researchers have proposed several explanations. One holds that children treat pronouns as fixed names, not as words that change with the speaker; Naima's data fit this account. Another holds that children may know how adults use them but speak from the other's point of view; Ethan's data partly support this account. Yet another says that children learn the second person from what adults say to others, and firstborn or only children have less chance to hear such speech; both children were firstborns with no siblings during the study. The authors conclude that both hypotheses receive only limited support.

**Bringing it into the framework.**

First choose the [cut](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c23): the [nodes](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c25) are the child and the parents; what the parents say is [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) that [arrives](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10) at the child, and what the child says is the child's [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28). The child at this stage is not assumed by this paper to be a [social person](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c03); by 6.1, there can still be [constructs](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) in her [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28): the framework does not assert that [second-order distinction](../paper_fb0addca_42b6_4884_aa18_438c695747ab/concepts.md#c01) or [view attribution](../paper_fb0addca_42b6_4884_aa18_438c695747ab/concepts.md#c02) is a necessary condition for the use of [constructs](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) (7.7).

- **The material comes from outside; the [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18) is one's own.** Parents say "do you want milk" to the child every day; these [outputs](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) [arrive](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10) at the child and are the child's material. The [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18) the child makes with this material is the child's own (6.2, note 1). The parents saying "you" aloud does not mean the child has obtained "you" as a [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01); the [arrival](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10) of a name is not the transfer of a [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) (7.1, note 1).
- **During the reversal period, "you" is already a [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01).** When Naima says "you have blueberries on your face", "you" appears in her [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) and points at an object: herself. By 6.1, this is a [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01), pointing at a fixed object, like a name.
- **"You" in adult usage points at a position.** "You" is used for one person spoken to after another, and what it points at is the position "the side spoken to". So the move from reversal to correct use is, in the framework, not a move from [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18) to [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01), but a change in what the [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) points at, from a fixed object to a position. This connects with the "treated as a name" explanation.
- **Hearing others talk to each other.** If the third account holds, the child hears, in what the parents say to each other, "you" pointing at different people. This is material; whether the child will therefore change what "you" points at is [not guaranteed](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c02) (6.4).
- **The abrupt stop.** Naima suddenly stopped reversing at two years five months. Both of the authors' speculations are the sources' attributions; by 6.4, when it would happen cannot be derived from an earlier point.

**What the [record](../paper_81952268_47b1_4d8c_9605_270c42646693/concepts.md#c06) contains and what cannot be derived.**

What the [record](../paper_81952268_47b1_4d8c_9605_270c42646693/concepts.md#c06) contains: in these [records](../paper_81952268_47b1_4d8c_9605_270c42646693/concepts.md#c06), some children use "you" for themselves for a period of time, and later switch to using it for the person spoken to; the change can be abrupt.

Cannot be derived: what [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18) the child made inside; which hypothesis is right: both hypotheses are researchers' attributions about the child's [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28), and by 6.5 confirming them from outside is [not guaranteed](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c02), which agrees with the authors' saying that both receive only limited support; and which [arrival](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10) the end of the reversals came from.

**Notes on the data.**

What happened is taken mainly from Evans and Demuth's 2012 paper in the Journal of Child Language, "Individual differences in pronoun reversal: Evidence from two longitudinal case studies", with the relevant passages extracted by a tool, not from the original document; the figures of Dale and Crain-Thoreson in it are as cited in that paper. Ethan's other background is irrelevant to this case and is not included.

<note>

*The first case: some children aged one or two swap "you" and "I", for example saying "you have blueberries on your face" to mean on their own face. A girl called Naima swapped for several months and suddenly stopped at two years five months.*

*Put into the framework: the "you" said by the parents reaches the child only as material; how the child draws lines is the child's own affair. A child of this age is not counted by this paper as a social person, but she can still have constructs. During the swapping, the child's "you" already points at a fixed person, namely herself, like a name; the "you" adults use points at the position "the person spoken to". So learning "you" is the construct switching from pointing at a person to pointing at a position. In the record one can see how she spoke and when she changed; one cannot see what she drew inside, nor which sentence made her change. The data are from a 2012 paper, extracted by a tool.*

</note>

### 12.2 Neptune and Vulcan

**What happened.**

In the first half of the nineteenth century, the actual position of Uranus kept departing from its calculated position. The French astronomer Le Verrier supposed that an unseen planet farther out was pulling it, and used Newtonian mechanics to work back to where this planet should be. On 23 September 1846, Galle at the Berlin Observatory searched for only an hour and found Neptune less than one degree from the position Le Verrier had calculated.

Le Verrier then studied Mercury. Mercury's perihelion slowly moves, but it actually moves a little faster than calculated. The masses of the planets were not yet measured accurately enough at the time, and Le Verrier calculated 38 arcseconds per century that could not be explained; the present value is 43 arcseconds per century. Using the same method, Le Verrier proposed a planet, or a group of small bodies, inside the orbit of Mercury.

In 1859, Lescarbault reported seeing a small black dot cross the Sun on 26 March, and Le Verrier received his letter on 22 December; on 2 January 1860, Le Verrier announced the discovery of Vulcan at the Academy of Sciences, publishing an orbit about 21 million kilometers from the Sun with a period of 19 days and 17 hours. After that, Le Verrier repeatedly adjusted Vulcan's orbital parameters in line with new sighting reports, many of which had no date or an inaccurate time; he published the dates of future transits, and when the predictions failed, he changed the parameters again. Astronomers searched during transits and total solar eclipses for decades.

From 1866 to 1878 there were no reliable observations. At the total solar eclipse of 29 July 1878, Watson and Swift both said they saw a planet near the Sun; Peters later held that it was a known star. Eclipse searches continued until 1908; in that year, Campbell and Perrine of the Lick Observatory held that, observationally, the question of an intra-Mercurial planet was closed. In 1915, Einstein's general theory of relativity explained the anomaly in Mercury's orbit without a hidden planet. The name was also used outside astronomy: a weather forecaster made forecasts based on Vulcan's supposed motion.

**Bringing it into the framework.**

First choose the [cut](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c23): in [case](concepts.md#c24) A, the [target](concepts.md#c25) is the region of sky near the position Le Verrier calculated, the [earlier material](concepts.md#c29) is the deviation in Uranus's position, the [inference](concepts.md#c36) is calculating from it where the unseen planet should be, and the [later material](concepts.md#c30) is the 1846 observation of that region of sky. In [case](concepts.md#c24) B, the [target](concepts.md#c25) is the region of sky near the Sun inside Mercury's orbit, the [earlier material](concepts.md#c29) is the deviation of Mercury's perihelion and the early sighting reports, the [inference](concepts.md#c36) is the transit predictions, and the [later material](concepts.md#c30) is the observation of the Sun after the predicted dates. In both cases the [compression](concepts.md#c31) turns observational [records](../paper_81952268_47b1_4d8c_9605_270c42646693/concepts.md#c06) into [representations](concepts.md#c34) by mass, position and velocity, plus the known bodies; the [inference](concepts.md#c36) is the calculation of celestial mechanics.

- **Question one: both are [constructs](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01).** When "that unseen planet" was calculated, no [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) from it that was recognized had [arrived](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10) at the astronomers; it pointed at an object not present, had a name and an orbit, and was written into letters and sent off: all three [functions](concepts.md#c18) were there (4.3). By the definition of [illusory construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01), calling them [constructs](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) neither claims nor denies that the objects exist; Neptune was found later and Vulcan was not, and before being found the two had the same standing.
- **Being [commutative](concepts.md#c55): the same method fits once and fails once.** In [case](concepts.md#c24) A, the [inferred](concepts.md#c36) position differed from the later observation by less than one degree; if the researcher states an [allowed error](concepts.md#c56) of one degree, this [comparison](concepts.md#c38) is [commutative on a case](concepts.md#c40), the case being this one. In [case](concepts.md#c24) B, the transit predictions repeatedly failed to fit the observations after the predicted dates: not [commutative](concepts.md#c55). That the same [compression](concepts.md#c31) and [inference](concepts.md#c36) are [commutative](concepts.md#c55) on one [case](concepts.md#c24) does not imply that they are [commutative](concepts.md#c55) on another [case](concepts.md#c24).
- **Readings can be wrong.** The two sightings of 1878 were researchers using the [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) Vulcan when [compressing](concepts.md#c31) [later material](concepts.md#c30), reading a point of light as Vulcan; it was later held that the reading was wrong. The outcome of a [comparison](concepts.md#c38) is relative to how the researcher [compresses](concepts.md#c31) this material (5.3).
- **Not [commutative](concepts.md#c55), and used all the same.** After the predictions failed, Le Verrier changed the parameters and predicted again. Disagreeing observations [arrived](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10) one after another and participated in [weight](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c06), and the [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) carrying the [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) did not stop appearing because of them (8.1, 8.2); it was even taken up in weather forecasting (8.4). Adding Vulcan only puts one more body into the same calculation of celestial mechanics: on the same [representation](concepts.md#c34) this calculation gives only one result, so by the definition of [self-consistent](concepts.md#c45) it is [self-consistent](concepts.md#c45); yet on [case](concepts.md#c24) B it is not [commutative](concepts.md#c55) (Proposition Six).
- **Withdrawal.** Around 1908 the astronomical community held that the matter was observationally closed, and after 1915 there was another [compression](concepts.md#c31). By 8.1, Vulcan's ceasing to be used does not follow necessarily from the disagreeing observations; why it was dropped at that time is the sources' attribution.

**What the [record](../paper_81952268_47b1_4d8c_9605_270c42646693/concepts.md#c06) contains and what cannot be derived.**

What the [record](../paper_81952268_47b1_4d8c_9605_270c42646693/concepts.md#c06) contains: with the same method, starting from the deviation of Uranus, the [inference](concepts.md#c36) agreed with later observation; starting from the deviation of Mercury, the transit predictions failed again and again, the parameters were adjusted again and again, and the search went on for decades; someone used the same [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) in weather forecasting.

Cannot be derived: what Le Verrier or other astronomers "believed", since the [record](../paper_81952268_47b1_4d8c_9605_270c42646693/concepts.md#c06) contains only papers, letters, predictions and announcements; why the search went on so long, and why it stopped when it did; how long Vulcan would have been used without general relativity.

**Notes on the data.**

What happened is taken from the Wikipedia article "Vulcan (hypothetical planet)", the Britannica article "Urbain-Jean-Joseph Le Verrier", and a biographical page on Le Verrier on the Book of Proofs website, with the relevant passages extracted by a tool, not from the original documents. Sources differ on who first proposed the name Vulcan: Wikipedia records it as proposed by Babinet in 1846, while another source records it as proposed by Le Verrier in 1859. For the excess of Mercury's perihelion, Le Verrier's calculation at the time was 38 arcseconds per century and the modern value is 43 arcseconds per century; both are given.

<note>

*The second case: Le Verrier used Newtonian mechanics to calculate from the deviation of Uranus that there should be an unseen planet farther out, and within an hour the Berlin Observatory found Neptune near the calculated position. With the same method he said there was also a planet inside Mercury's orbit, called Vulcan, but it could not be found for decades; the predictions failed again and again and the parameters were changed again and again, until in 1915 general relativity explained Mercury's orbit without this planet.*

*Put into the framework: before being found, both planets were constructs, both pointing at something not in front of one, with the same standing. The same method fitted once and failed once. Reading a point of light as Vulcan is a reading that can be wrong; whether a comparison fits depends on how the researcher reads that observation. Not fitting, it was still used for decades, and was even taken up for weather forecasting. In the record one can see papers, letters and predictions; one cannot see what the astronomers believed inside, nor derive why it was given up when it was. The data come from Wikipedia, Britannica and a biographical page.*

</note>

---

## 13. General Proposition

**General Proposition**: For a mere physical [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) to become an [illusory construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01), it goes through two steps: from dependence to [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18), and from [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18) to [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01). Without [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18), there is no [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) that is the content of its [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18); from there being [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18), it does not follow that there is a [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01). [Constructs](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) and [mere distinction](concepts.md#c19) both participate in later [weight](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c06); the difference is that a [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) has an object it points to. Only with an object pointed to are there [reference](concepts.md#c20) to what is not present, [inference](concepts.md#c36), and [transmission between Wo](concepts.md#c21) through [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) that points at it.

If the content of a [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16)'s [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18) becomes a [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01), the pathway is: the [writing](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c29) of a [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18) [arrives](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10) again through a [small loop](../paper_2ccbf179_58cf_4851_bd72_5fa886e4b2c3/concepts.md#c02), its content is used to point at some object, and afterwards it may be [lumped](concepts.md#c23) again; it is not claimed that every step must be passed through, nor that the order is fixed. Among the necessary conditions, [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18) is derived from definitions. The [Self-Configuration Assumption](concepts.md#c04) has necessary conditions of its own, whose engineering conditions are [empirical premises](concepts.md#c01); this paper does not list these engineering conditions as necessary conditions for the generation of [constructs](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01). With all conditions present, generation is [not guaranteed](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c02); once generated, confirmation from outside is also [not guaranteed](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c02); on this, the judgments this paper gives from outside are only negations.

The use of [constructs](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) has two pathways: inside a [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) and between [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16). Between [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16), what [arrives](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10) is the [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) that carries it, not a transfer of the [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01); from obtaining the same [template](../paper_2ccbf179_58cf_4851_bd72_5fa886e4b2c3/concepts.md#c14), it cannot be derived that the obtainers' execution is the same, and the consistency of execution the researcher can register lies only in [outputs](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28); when the [outputs](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) [arrive](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10) at one another within stated dimensions and time windows and repeatedly follow on compatibly, it is the [resonance](../paper_2ccbf179_58cf_4851_bd72_5fa886e4b2c3/concepts.md#c09) of [outputs](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28).

When [inferring](concepts.md#c36) with [constructs](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01), the criterion is whether the [inference](concepts.md#c36) is [commutative within a range](concepts.md#c42) that is stated. Relying only on the same [representation](concepts.md#c34), lost differences cannot be recovered; however high the [operation capacity](concepts.md#c14), it cannot make up differences lost in containment; [comparison](concepts.md#c38) reaches only [cases](concepts.md#c24) that have [arrived](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10); for a [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) not [locked in](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c12) whose differing results fall on the [representation](concepts.md#c34) being [compared](concepts.md#c38), being [commutative](concepts.md#c55) in a single result is [not guaranteed](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c02); [self-consistency](concepts.md#c45) does not imply being [commutative](concepts.md#c55), and being [commutative](concepts.md#c55) does not imply [self-consistency](concepts.md#c45). A [node](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c25) whose [rounds](../paper_fb0addca_42b6_4884_aa18_438c695747ab/concepts.md#c04) are [computation](../paper_fb0addca_42b6_4884_aa18_438c695747ab/concepts.md#c05) need not make [distinctions](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18); what reads [computation](../paper_fb0addca_42b6_4884_aa18_438c695747ab/concepts.md#c05) as [inference](concepts.md#c36) is the [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) that reads it. When a [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) is not [commutative](concepts.md#c55), disagreeing [arrivals](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10) still participate in [weight](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c06), but whether it therefore ceases to be used is [not guaranteed](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c02); continued use is not conditioned on being [commutative](concepts.md#c55).

[Current artificial intelligence](concepts.md#c11) is not a [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) under the logical-state [cut](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c23), has no [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18), and has no [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) that is the content of [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18); on certain dimensions stated by the researcher, if the effects stated depend only on the content of [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) on these dimensions, two [outputs](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) that are the same on these dimensions have the same effects when they [arrive](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10) at the same [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16), and in this sense it can be [functionally equivalent](concepts.md#c52) to a [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16); on dimensions where [output](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c28) cannot be distinguished, it is a [simulation](concepts.md#c51) of a [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16). Between [locked-in](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c12) [nodes](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c25) of the same [structure](concepts.md#c07) whose constraints are determined only by [structure](concepts.md#c07) and state, the way of processing [arrivals](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c10) can be passed on completely by copying; between [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) with different earlier [histories](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c13), it cannot be derived that this can be done.

As for when a given [Wo](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c16) makes a [distinction](../paper_37b3643a_dc40_4463_8b36_4f4594acd986/concepts.md#c18), when a given content becomes a [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01), and which consequence follows after a [construct](../paper_08383b0a_1167_4efb_9b89_dc8a71afd5ff/concepts.md#c01) is used, this paper derives none of them uniquely.

(Following the way of writing in 1.2, summarized from the conclusions of 4.2, 4.3, 5.3, Propositions One to Six of 5.4, 6.1 to 6.5, 7.1, 7.3, 8.1, 8.2, 9.1, 9.3 and 9.6. The parts relying on Proposition Four, 6.4 and 7.3 hold under the [Application Assumption](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c50); the parts relying on [Empirical Premises](concepts.md#c01) One to Five lose their source when these premises are overturned by new cases; the parts relying on 9.2 hold under the logical-state [cut](../paper_b9df3b68_23ee_4839_965b_ed0e6b283ee6/concepts.md#c23); the parts relying on 9.1 and 9.6 hold under this paper's settings about humans and machines listed in Section 10.)

<note>

*Summary: for a thing's mere physical response to become a construct, there must first be distinction, and then the content of the distinction is used to point at something. Both affect what comes after; the only difference is whether something is pointed at. Only with something pointed at can one think of what is not in front of one, infer with it, and tell others. If content a thing draws itself becomes a construct, it may be content once drawn coming back to oneself, being used to point at something, and afterwards being grouped again, not necessarily in this order or through every step; with all conditions present it is not guaranteed to arise, and if it arises it is not guaranteed to be visible from outside; the judgments this paper gives from outside are only ruling out.*

*Constructs can be used on oneself, and also between people through what is said aloud; what passes over is words, not the construct itself. Inferring with a construct and fitting is called commutative; lost differences cannot be made up, only what has been received can be compared, a single prediction about a person is not guaranteed accurate, and being free of internal contradiction and fitting what arrives later are two different things. Slide rules and artificial intelligence are only calculating; what reads them as inferring is the Wo that reads them, usually a person. A construct that does not fit is not necessarily dropped. Today's artificial intelligence is not a Wo, but in some respects its effects are the same as a person's and its output cannot be told apart; machines can be copied whole when their rules depend only on structure and state, for people with different experiences, this paper cannot derive that they can. Who will draw a distinction when, when a construct will arise, and what will happen after it is used: this paper cannot derive definite answers to any of these.*

</note>
